  435      st.divider()
  436      st.subheader("Today vs Yesterday")
  437      full = load_log()
  438      if not full.empty:
  439          yest_str = str(date.today() - timedelta(days=1))
  440          yest_df = full[full["date"].astype(str) == yest_str]
  441          td = full[full["date"].astype(str) == today_str]
  442  
  443          if not yest_df.empty and not td.empty:
  444              yest_dom = int(yest_df["reduced"].value_counts().index[0])
  445              today_dom = int(td["reduced"].value_counts().index[0])
  446  
  447              dc1, dc2 = st.columns(2)
  448              dc1.metric("Yesterday's Dominant Reduced", yest_dom)
  449              dc2.metric("Today's Dominant Reduced", today_dom,
  450                          delta="changed" if today_dom != yest_dom else "same")
  451  
  452              # Repeating ordinals across days
  453              shared_ords = set(yest_df["ordinal"]).intersection(set(td["ordinal"]))
  454              if shared_ords:
  455                  st.write(f"**Repeating ordinals across both days:** {', '.join(str(x) for x in sorted(shared_ords))}")
  456              else:
  457                  st.write("No ordinals repeat across yesterday and today.")
  458  
  459              st.write(f"Yesterday: {len(yest_df)} entries, Today: {len(td)} entries")
  460          elif yest_df.empty:
  461              st.caption("No data from yesterday to compare.")
  462          else:
  463              st.caption("No data for today yet.")
  464      else:
  465          st.caption("No log data available.")
  466  
  467  
  468  # ===================== TAB 2: HISTORY =====================
  469  with tab_history:
  470      st.header("Scan History")
  471      hist_df = load_log()
  472      if not hist_df.empty:
  473          st.dataframe(hist_df.sort_index(ascending=False), use_container_width=True)
  474          st.caption(f"Total entries: {len(hist_df)}")
  475      else:
  476          st.caption("No entries yet.")
  477  
  478      # 30-day trend
  479      st.divider()
  480      st.subheader("30-Day Trend")
  481      if not hist_df.empty:
  482          cutoff = str(date.today() - timedelta(days=30))
  483          recent = hist_df[hist_df["date"].astype(str) >= cutoff]
  484          if not recent.empty:
  485              dates = recent["date"].astype(str).unique()
  486              day_dominants = {}
  487              for d in sorted(dates):
  488                  day_data = recent[recent["date"].astype(str) == d]
  489                  dom = int(day_data["reduced"].value_counts().index[0])
  490                  day_dominants[d] = dom
  491  
  492              dom_counter = Counter(day_dominants.values())
  493              st.write(f"**Data spans {len(dates)} day(s) in the last 30 days**")
  494              for val, cnt in dom_counter.most_common(3):
  495                  st.write(f"- Reduced **{val}** dominated **{cnt}** of {len(dates)} days")
  496  
  497              # Show daily dominant as a simple table
  498              trend_df = pd.DataFrame(
  499                  [{"Date": d, "Dominant Reduced": v} for d, v in day_dominants.items()]
  500              )
  501              st.dataframe(trend_df, use_container_width=True)
  502          else:
  503              st.caption("No data in the last 30 days.")
  504      else:
  505          st.caption("No log data available.")
  506  
  507  
  508  # ===================== TAB 3: PATTERNS =====================
  509  with tab_patterns:
  510      st.header("Pattern Detection")
  511      pat_df = load_log()
  512  
  513      if not pat_df.empty and len(pat_df) >= 2:
  514          # Repeating ordinals
  515          ord_counts = pat_df["ordinal"].value_counts()
  516          repeats = ord_counts[ord_counts >= 2]
  517          st.write("**Repeating Ordinals** (appearing 2+ times)")
  518          if not repeats.empty:
  519              for val, cnt in repeats.head(15).items():
  520                  st.write(f"- Ordinal **{val}** appeared **{cnt}** times")
  521          else:
  522              st.write("- No repeating ordinals yet")
  523  
  524          # Dominant reduced
  525          red_counts = pat_df["reduced"].value_counts()
  526          st.write("**Reduced Value Distribution**")
  527          r1, r2 = st.columns(2)
  528          r1.metric("Most Common Reduced", int(red_counts.index[0]))
  529          r2.metric("Frequency", f"{red_counts.iloc[0] / len(pat_df):.0%} ({red_counts.iloc[0]}/{len(pat_df)})")
  530  
  531          # Score distribution
  532          high_c = (pat_df["score"] >= 70).sum()
  533          med_c = ((pat_df["score"] >= 40) & (pat_df["score"] < 70)).sum()
  534          low_c = (pat_df["score"] < 40).sum()
  535          st.write("**Score Distribution**")
  536          d1, d2, d3 = st.columns(3)
  537          d1.metric("HIGH", int(high_c))
  538          d2.metric("MEDIUM", int(med_c))
  539          d3.metric("LOW", int(low_c))
  540  
  541          # Gematria signal reading
  542          st.divider()
  543          st.subheader("Gematria Signal")
  544          repeat_count = (ord_counts >= 2).sum()
  545          top_red = int(red_counts.index[0])
  546          top_red_pct = red_counts.iloc[0] / len(pat_df)
  547  
  548          if high_c >= 3:
  549              field = "HIGH COHERENCE"
  550          elif high_c >= 1:
  551              field = "MODERATE ACTIVITY"
  552          elif med_c > low_c:
  553              field = "LOW-MODERATE ACTIVITY"
  554          else:
  555              field = "LOW ACTIVITY"
  556  
  557          obs = []
  558          if high_c >= 2:
  559              obs.append(f"Multiple high-value alignments ({high_c} HIGH signals)")
  560          elif high_c == 1:
  561              obs.append("Isolated high signal detected")
  562          else:
  563              obs.append("No strong numerical alignment")
  564  
  565          if repeat_count >= 3:
  566              obs.append(f"Repeating ordinal structures ({repeat_count} values repeat)")
  567          elif repeat_count >= 1:
  568              obs.append(f"Repetition present ({repeat_count} value(s) repeat)")
  569          else:
  570              obs.append("No ordinal repetition")
  571  
  572          if top_red_pct > 0.30:
  573              obs.append(f"Reduced clustering (reduced {top_red} at {top_red_pct:.0%})")
  574          elif top_red_pct > 0.20:
  575              obs.append(f"Mild concentration (reduced {top_red} at {top_red_pct:.0%})")
  576          else:
  577              obs.append("Reduced values evenly distributed")
  578  
  579          interp_map = {
  580              "HIGH COHERENCE": "Pattern convergence — multiple signals aligning",
  581              "MODERATE ACTIVITY": "Emerging signal — monitor for escalation",
  582              "LOW-MODERATE ACTIVITY": "Noise with occasional alignment",
  583              "LOW ACTIVITY": "No meaningful pattern structure detected",
  584          }
  585          obs_lines = "\n".join(f"  - {o}" for o in obs)
  586          st.code(
  587              f"GEMATRIA SIGNAL\n\n"
  588              f"Field: {field}\n\n"
  589              f"{obs_lines}\n\n"
  590              f"-> Interpretation: {interp_map[field]}",
  591              language=None,
  592          )
  593  
  594          # All-time cross-source matches
  595          st.divider()
  596          st.subheader("Cross-Source Matches (All Time)")
  597          all_xm = find_cross_source_matches(pat_df)
  598          if all_xm:
  599              for xm in all_xm:
  600                  st.write(f"**{xm['date']} — {xm['type'].title()} {xm['value']}** — {', '.join(xm['sources'])}")
  601                  for h in xm["headlines"]:
  602                      st.write(f"  - {h}")
  603          else:
  604              st.caption("No cross-source matches found.")
  605      else:
  606          st.caption("Need at least 2 entries to detect patterns.")
  607  
  608  
  609  # ===================== TAB 4: CONTROL GROUP =====================
  610  with tab_control:
  611      st.header("Control Group Analysis")
  612      st.caption("Generate random words and compare their gematria distribution to your scans.")
  613  
  614      n_words = st.slider("Number of random words:", 50, 500, 100, step=50, key="ctrl_n")
  615      word_len = st.slider("Word length:", 3, 12, 6, key="ctrl_len")
  616  
  617      if st.button("Generate Control Group", key="ctrl_btn"):
  618          ctrl_df = run_control_group(n=n_words, word_length=word_len)
  619  
  620          col1, col2 = st.columns(2)
  621          with col1:
  622              st.subheader("Control: Ordinal Distribution")
  623              st.bar_chart(ctrl_df["ordinal"].value_counts().sort_index())
  624          with col2:
  625              st.subheader("Control: Reduced Distribution")
  626              st.bar_chart(ctrl_df["reduced"].value_counts().sort_index())
  627  
  628          st.divider()
  629          cmp_df = load_log()
  630          if not cmp_df.empty:
  631              st.subheader("Your Scans vs Control")
  632              cc1, cc2 = st.columns(2)
  633              with cc1:
  634                  st.metric("Your avg ordinal", f"{cmp_df['ordinal'].mean():.1f}")
  635                  st.metric("Control avg ordinal", f"{ctrl_df['ordinal'].mean():.1f}")
  636              with cc2:
  637                  st.metric("Your avg reduced", f"{cmp_df['reduced'].mean():.1f}")
  638                  st.metric("Control avg reduced", f"{ctrl_df['reduced'].mean():.1f}")
  639  
  640          st.divider()
  641          st.subheader("Sample Data")
  642          st.dataframe(ctrl_df.head(20), use_container_width=True)

================================================================================
  FILE: liquidation_monitor.py
================================================================================

    1  """
    2  Liquidation data monitor – tracks crypto liquidation events.
    3  
    4  Uses the CoinGlass public API for aggregated liquidation data.
    5  Falls back to Coingecko derivatives data if CoinGlass is unavailable.
    6  """
    7  from __future__ import annotations
    8  
    9  import logging
   10  import time
   11  
   12  import requests
   13  
   14  import ai_writer
   15  
   16  logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   17  
   18  # CoinGlass public endpoints (no API key required for basic data)
   19  _COINGLASS_LIQMAP_URL = "https://open-api.coinglass.com/public/v2/liquidation_history"
   20  _COINGLASS_LIQINFO_URL = "https://open-api.coinglass.com/public/v2/liquidation_info"
   21  
   22  # Fallback: aggregate from exchange APIs
   23  _CACHE: dict = {}
   24  _CACHE_TTL = 300  # 5 min cache
   25  
   26  
   27  def _fetch_coinglass_liquidations() -> dict | None:
   28      """
   29      Fetch aggregated liquidation data from CoinGlass public API.
   30      Returns dict with total_liq_24h, long_liq_24h, short_liq_24h, etc.
   31      """
   32      # Try the public liquidation endpoint
   33      for attempt in range(3):
   34          try:
   35              resp = requests.get(
   36                  "https://open-api.coinglass.com/public/v2/liquidation_history",
   37                  params={"time_type": 2, "symbol": "all"},
   38                  timeout=15,
   39              )
   40              if resp.status_code == 429:
   41                  time.sleep(2 ** (attempt + 1))
   42                  continue
   43              if resp.status_code != 200:
   44                  break
   45              data = resp.json()
   46              if data.get("code") == "0" and data.get("data"):
   47                  return data["data"]
   48          except requests.RequestException as exc:
   49              logger.warning("CoinGlass fetch failed (attempt %d/3): %s", attempt + 1, exc)
   50              if attempt < 2:
   51                  time.sleep(2 ** (attempt + 1))
   52      return None
   53  
   54  
   55  def _fetch_derivative_data() -> dict | None:
   56      """
   57      Fallback: fetch derivatives/open interest data from CoinGecko.
   58      """
   59      try:
   60          resp = requests.get(
   61              "https://api.coingecko.com/api/v3/derivatives",
   62              timeout=15,
   63          )
   64          resp.raise_for_status()
   65          data = resp.json()
   66          if not data:
   67              return None
   68  
   69          # Aggregate some useful stats
   70          total_oi = 0
   71          total_volume = 0
   72          funding_rates = []
   73  
   74          for d in data[:20]:
   75              oi = d.get("open_interest") or 0
   76              vol = d.get("h24_volume") or 0
   77              total_oi += oi
   78              total_volume += vol
   79              fr = d.get("funding_rate")
   80              if fr is not None:
   81                  try:
   82                      funding_rates.append(float(fr))
   83                  except (ValueError, TypeError):
   84                      pass
   85  
   86          avg_funding = sum(funding_rates) / len(funding_rates) if funding_rates else 0
   87  
   88          return {
   89              "total_open_interest": total_oi,
   90              "total_volume_24h": total_volume,
   91              "avg_funding_rate": avg_funding,
   92              "exchanges_count": len(data),
   93          }
   94      except requests.RequestException as exc:
   95          logger.warning("CoinGecko derivatives fetch failed: %s", exc)
   96          return None
   97  
   98  
   99  def fetch_liquidation_data() -> dict | None:
  100      """
  101      Fetch liquidation/derivatives data from available sources.
  102      Returns normalized dict with whatever data we could gather.
  103      """
  104      now = time.time()
  105  
  106      # Check cache
  107      if _CACHE and (now - _CACHE.get("_ts", 0)) < _CACHE_TTL:
  108          return _CACHE
  109  
  110      # Try CoinGlass first
  111      cg_data = _fetch_coinglass_liquidations()
  112      if cg_data:
  113          result = {"source": "coinglass", "raw": cg_data, "_ts": now}
  114          _CACHE.update(result)
  115          return result
  116  
  117      # Fallback to CoinGecko derivatives
  118      deriv = _fetch_derivative_data()
  119      if deriv:
  120          result = {"source": "derivatives", **deriv, "_ts": now}
  121          _CACHE.update(result)
  122          return result
  123  
  124      return None
  125  
  126  
  127  def _fmt_usd(val: float) -> str:
  128      """Format large USD values."""
  129      if val >= 1e9:
  130          return f"${val / 1e9:.1f}B"
  131      if val >= 1e6:
  132          return f"${val / 1e6:.0f}M"
  133      if val >= 1e3:
  134          return f"${val / 1e3:.0f}K"
  135      return f"${val:.0f}"
  136  
  137  
  138  def format_liquidation_tweet(data: dict) -> str | None:
  139      """Generate a liquidation/derivatives tweet from available data."""
  140      source = data.get("source", "")
  141  
  142      if source == "coinglass" and data.get("raw"):
  143          raw = data["raw"]
  144          # CoinGlass data format varies — handle list or dict
  145          if isinstance(raw, list) and raw:
  146              entry = raw[0] if isinstance(raw[0], dict) else {}
  147              long_liq = entry.get("longLiquidationUsd", 0) or 0
  148              short_liq = entry.get("shortLiquidationUsd", 0) or 0
  149              total = long_liq + short_liq
  150  
  151              if total < 1_000_000:
  152                  return None  # Not interesting enough
  153  
  154              if ai_writer.is_available():
  155                  prompt = f"""Write a tweet about crypto liquidations in the last 24 hours.
  156  
  157  Liquidation data:
  158  - Total liquidated: {_fmt_usd(total)}
  159  - Longs liquidated: {_fmt_usd(long_liq)}
  160  - Shorts liquidated: {_fmt_usd(short_liq)}
  161  - {'Longs got wrecked harder' if long_liq > short_liq else 'Shorts got squeezed harder'}
  162  
  163  The tweet should:
  164  - Lead with the total liquidation number — it's dramatic
  165  - Note which side (longs or shorts) got hit harder
  166  - Explain what this means for the market structure
  167  - Sound like a trader watching the carnage, not a news report
  168  - Under 270 characters
  169  - NO hashtags
  170  {ai_writer._get_recent_context()}
  171  Write the tweet now. Nothing else."""
  172  
  173                  system = """You are @CoinWatchAlert. Liquidation events are your bread and butter — you call them out in real-time with sharp commentary. No hashtags, no filler."""
  174                  tweet = ai_writer._call_claude(system, prompt)
  175                  if tweet and len(tweet) <= 275:
  176                      return tweet
  177  
  178              # Template fallback
  179              dominant = "longs" if long_liq > short_liq else "shorts"
  180              emoji = "🔴" if long_liq > short_liq else "🟢"
  181              return (
  182                  f"💥 {_fmt_usd(total)} liquidated in 24h\n\n"
  183                  f"→ Longs: {_fmt_usd(long_liq)}\n"
  184                  f"→ Shorts: {_fmt_usd(short_liq)}\n\n"
  185                  f"{emoji} {'Bears winning' if long_liq > short_liq else 'Bulls winning'} — "
  186                  f"{dominant} getting wiped out."
  187              )
  188  
  189      if source == "derivatives":
  190          oi = data.get("total_open_interest", 0)
  191          vol = data.get("total_volume_24h", 0)
  192          funding = data.get("avg_funding_rate", 0)
  193  
  194          if oi <= 0 and vol <= 0:
  195              return None
  196  
  197          if ai_writer.is_available():
  198              funding_str = f"{funding:.4f}%" if funding else "neutral"
  199              prompt = f"""Write a tweet about crypto derivatives/leverage data.
  200  
  201  Derivatives data:
  202  - Total open interest: {_fmt_usd(oi)}
  203  - 24h derivatives volume: {_fmt_usd(vol)}
  204  - Average funding rate: {funding_str}
  205  - {'Positive funding = longs paying shorts (market leaning bullish)' if funding > 0 else 'Negative funding = shorts paying longs (market leaning bearish)' if funding < 0 else 'Neutral funding = market balanced'}
  206  
  207  The tweet should:
  208  - Focus on what the leverage data tells us about positioning
  209  - If funding is extreme (>0.03% or <-0.03%), flag it as a potential reversal signal
  210  - Sound like a trader who watches the derivatives market closely
  211  - Under 270 characters
  212  - NO hashtags
  213  {ai_writer._get_recent_context()}
  214  Write the tweet now. Nothing else."""
  215  
  216              system = """You are @CoinWatchAlert. You track derivatives data obsessively — open interest, funding rates, and leverage tell you where the crowd is positioned. No hashtags, no filler."""
  217              tweet = ai_writer._call_claude(system, prompt)
  218              if tweet and len(tweet) <= 275:
  219                  return tweet
  220  
  221          # Template fallback
  222          lines = ["📊 Derivatives snapshot:"]
  223          lines.append("")
  224          if oi > 0:
  225              lines.append(f"→ Open interest: {_fmt_usd(oi)}")
  226          if vol > 0:
  227              lines.append(f"→ 24h volume: {_fmt_usd(vol)}")
  228          if funding:
  229              direction = "longs pay" if funding > 0 else "shorts pay"
  230              lines.append(f"→ Avg funding: {funding:.4f}% ({direction})")
  231              if abs(funding) > 0.03:
  232                  lines.extend(["", "⚡ Extreme funding — watch for a squeeze."])
  233  
  234          tweet = "\n".join(lines)
  235          if len(tweet) > 275:
  236              tweet = tweet[:272].rsplit("\n", 1)[0] + "…"
  237          return tweet
  238  
  239      return None


================================================================================
  FILE: news_api.py
================================================================================

    1  """
    2  NewsAPI.org client for fetching crypto news.
    3  Sign up at https://newsapi.org/ to get an API key.
    4  """
    5  from __future__ import annotations
    6  
    7  import logging
    8  from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta
    9  
   10  import os
   11  import feedparser
   12  import requests
   13  from dotenv import load_dotenv
   14  
   15  load_dotenv()
   16  
   17  NEWS_API_KEY = os.getenv("NEWS_API_KEY", "")
   18  NEWS_API_URL = "https://newsapi.org/v2/everything"
   19  
   20  logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   21  
   22  _JUNK_SOURCES = ("pypi", "beehiiv", "substack")
   23  
   24  _CRYPTO_KEYWORDS = (
   25      "bitcoin", "btc", "ethereum", "eth", "crypto", "blockchain",
   26      "defi", "nft", "altcoin", "binance", "coinbase", "stablecoin",
   27      "web3", "satoshi", "halving", "mining", "wallet", "token",
   28      "sec", "etf", "regulation",
   29  )
   30  
   31  
   32  def _is_junk(title: str, source_name: str) -> bool:
   33      source_lower = source_name.lower()
   34      if any(s in source_lower for s in _JUNK_SOURCES):
   35          return True
   36      title_lower = title.lower()
   37      if not any(kw in title_lower for kw in _CRYPTO_KEYWORDS):
   38          return True
   39      return False
   40  
   41  
   42  def fetch_crypto_news() -> list[dict]:
   43      """
   44      Fetch recent crypto articles from NewsAPI.org.
   45  
   46      Returns a list of dicts with keys:
   47          title, url, source, published_at
   48      Junk sources (pypi, beehiiv, substack) and non-crypto titles are excluded.
   49      Dedup against already-posted stories is handled by news_monitor._posted_hashes.
   50      Falls back gracefully to empty list if NEWS_API_KEY is missing.
   51      """
   52      if not NEWS_API_KEY:
   53          logger.debug("NEWS_API_KEY not set — skipping NewsAPI fetch")
   54          return []
   55      params = {
   56          "q": "bitcoin OR ethereum OR crypto OR blockchain",
   57          "language": "en",
   58          "sortBy": "publishedAt",
   59          "pageSize": 20,
   60          "apiKey": NEWS_API_KEY,
   61      }
   62      try:
   63          resp = requests.get(NEWS_API_URL, params=params, timeout=15)
   64          resp.raise_for_status()
   65          articles = resp.json().get("articles", [])
   66      except requests.RequestException as exc:
   67          logger.warning("NewsAPI fetch failed: %s", exc)
   68          return []
   69  
   70      results: list[dict] = []
   71      for article in articles:
   72          title = article.get("title", "").strip()
   73          source_name = (article.get("source") or {}).get("name", "")
   74          if not title or _is_junk(title, source_name):
   75              continue
   76          results.append({
   77              "title": title,
   78              "url": article.get("url", ""),
   79              "source": source_name,
   80              "published_at": article.get("publishedAt", ""),
   81          })
   82  
   83      return results
   84  
   85  
   86  _RSS_FEEDS = [
   87      ("https://cointelegraph.com/rss", "CoinTelegraph"),
   88      ("https://decrypt.co/feed", "Decrypt"),
   89      ("https://bitcoinmagazine.com/.rss/full/", "Bitcoin Magazine"),
   90      ("https://cryptoslate.com/feed/", "CryptoSlate"),
   91      ("https://cryptobriefing.com/feed/", "Crypto Briefing"),
   92      ("https://thedefiant.io/feed", "The Defiant"),
   93      ("https://www.theblock.co/rss.xml", "The Block"),
   94      ("https://cryptopotato.com/feed/", "CryptoPotato"),
   95      ("https://ambcrypto.com/feed/", "AMBCrypto"),
   96      ("https://newsbtc.com/feed/", "NewsBTC"),
   97      ("https://coinjournal.net/feed/", "CoinJournal"),
   98      ("https://u.today/rss", "U.Today"),
   99      ("https://beincrypto.com/feed/", "BeInCrypto"),
  100  ]
  101  
  102  
  103  def fetch_rss_news() -> list[dict]:
  104      """
  105      Fetch recent crypto articles from RSS feeds.
  106  
  107      Returns a list of dicts with keys:
  108          title, url, source, published_at
  109      Entries older than 3 hours are excluded. Results are deduplicated by URL.
  110      """
  111      cutoff = datetime.now(timezone.utc) - timedelta(hours=3)
  112      seen_urls: set[str] = set()
  113      results: list[dict] = []
  114  
  115      for feed_url, default_source in _RSS_FEEDS:
  116          try:
  117              feed = feedparser.parse(feed_url)
  118              if feed.bozo and not feed.entries:
  119                  logger.warning("RSS parse error for %s: %s", feed_url, feed.bozo_exception)
  120                  continue
  121              source = feed.feed.get("title", default_source)
  122              for entry in feed.entries:
  123                  url = entry.get("link", "")
  124                  if not url or url in seen_urls:
  125                      continue
  126                  title = entry.get("title", "").strip()
  127                  if not title:
  128                      continue
  129                  published_parsed = entry.get("published_parsed")
  130                  if published_parsed:
  131                      published_dt = datetime(*published_parsed[:6], tzinfo=timezone.utc)
  132                      if published_dt < cutoff:
  133                          continue
  134                      published_at = published_dt.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")
  135                  else:
  136                      published_at = ""
  137                  seen_urls.add(url)
  138                  results.append({
  139                      "title": title,
  140                      "url": url,
  141                      "source": source,
  142                      "published_at": published_at,
  143                  })
  144          except Exception as exc:
  145              logger.warning("RSS fetch failed for %s: %s", feed_url, exc)
  146  
  147      return results


================================================================================
  FILE: news_monitor.py
================================================================================

    1  """
    2  News monitor – polls RSS feeds and NewsAPI.org for hot/important crypto news
    3  and returns story objects that haven't been posted yet.
    4  """
    5  from __future__ import annotations
    6  
    7  import json
    8  import os
    9  import re
   10  import tempfile
   11  import time
   12  import hashlib
   13  import logging
   14  import requests
   15  import feedparser
   16  from bs4 import BeautifulSoup
   17  
   18  import config
   19  import ai_writer
   20  import news_api
   21  
   22  logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   23  
   24  # ── Noise patterns (pre-AI fast filter) ──────────────────────────────────────
   25  _NOISE_PATTERNS = [
   26      r'\bsponsored\b',
   27      r'\bpress release\b',
   28      r'\bpartnership\b.*\bannounce',
   29      r'\bgiveaway\b',
   30      r'\bairdrop\b',
   31      r'\bprice prediction\b',
   32      r'\bwill.*reach\b',
   33      r'\bcould.*hit\b',
   34      r'\btarget.*\$\d',
   35      r'\bshib\b.*\bmoon\b',
   36      r'top \d+ coins? to buy',
   37      r'best crypto.*\d{4}',
   38  ]
   39  
   40  _IMPORTANT_KEYWORDS = [
   41      "etf", "sec", "cftc", "fed", "federal reserve", "interest rate",
   42      "hack", "exploit", "breach", "stolen", "liquidat",
   43      "bitcoin", "btc", "ethereum", "eth",
   44      "institutional", "blackrock", "fidelity", "coinbase",
   45      "ban", "regulation", "legal", "lawsuit", "arrest",
   46      "halving", "ath", "all-time high",
   47      "stablecoin", "usdt", "usdc",
   48      "defi", "tvl", "bridge",
   49      "exchange", "binance", "ftx", "kraken",
   50      "whale", "inflow", "outflow",
   51  ]
   52  
   53  _MACRO_CRYPTO_BRIDGE_KEYWORDS = [
   54      "bitcoin", "crypto", "btc", "ethereum", "digital asset",
   55      "risk asset", "risk-off", "liquidity", "fed", "rate",
   56      "inflation", "dollar", "dxy", "yield", "treasury",
   57      "sanctions", "tariff",
   58  ]
   59  
   60  
   61  def _is_priority_source(story: dict) -> bool:
   62      """Return True if the story comes from a priority source."""
   63      source = story.get("source", "").lower()
   64      return any(ps in source for ps in _PRIORITY_SOURCES)
   65  
   66  
   67  def _pick_news_prefix(score: int, title: str) -> str:
   68      """
   69      Two-tier breaking news prefix.
   70      ⚡ BREAKING: — critical events (score 9+, hacks, regulatory actions, major exchange news)
   71      🚨 JUST IN:  — all other qualifying news (score 7-8)
   72      """
   73      title_lower = title.lower()
   74      is_critical = (
   75          score >= 9
   76          or any(w in title_lower for w in [
   77              "hack", "exploit", "stolen", "breach", "bankrupt", "collapse",
   78              "ban", "sec", "cftc", "lawsuit", "arrest", "sanction",
   79              "ath", "all-time high",
   80          ])
   81      )
   82      return "⚡ BREAKING:" if is_critical else "🚨 JUST IN:"
   83  
   84  # Story hashes we've already seen (persisted across restarts)
   85  _POSTED_HASHES_FILE = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), ".news_seen.json")
   86  _posted_hashes: dict[str, float] = {}   # hash -> timestamp when first seen
   87  
   88  
   89  def _load_posted_hashes() -> None:
   90      global _posted_hashes
   91      try:
   92          with open(_POSTED_HASHES_FILE) as f:
   93              data = json.load(f)
   94          _posted_hashes = data if isinstance(data, dict) else {}
   95      except (FileNotFoundError, json.JSONDecodeError):
   96          _posted_hashes = {}
   97  
   98  
   99  def _save_posted_hashes() -> None:
  100      try:
  101          with open(_POSTED_HASHES_FILE, "w") as f:
  102              json.dump(_posted_hashes, f)
  103      except OSError as exc:
  104          logger.warning("Could not save news seen hashes: %s", exc)
  105  
  106  
  107  _load_posted_hashes()
  108  
  109  
  110  def _story_hash(story: dict) -> str:
  111      """Stable identifier for a story based on its URL."""
  112      return hashlib.md5(story.get("url", story.get("title", "")).encode()).hexdigest()
  113  
  114  
  115  def _prune_old_hashes() -> None:
  116      """Remove hashes older than NEWS_DEDUP_WINDOW to keep memory bounded."""
  117      cutoff = time.time() - config.NEWS_DEDUP_WINDOW
  118      to_delete = [h for h, ts in _posted_hashes.items() if ts < cutoff]
  119      for h in to_delete:
  120          del _posted_hashes[h]
  121      if to_delete:
  122          _save_posted_hashes()
  123  
  124  
  125  # ── RSS feeds (primary source) ────────────────────────────────────────────────
  126  
  127  _RSS_FEEDS = [
  128      "https://cointelegraph.com/rss",
  129      "https://decrypt.co/feed",
  130      "https://www.coinbureau.com/feed/",
  131  ]
  132  
  133  # Sources that get a +1 score boost and are never deprioritised
  134  _PRIORITY_SOURCES = [
  135      "cointelegraph", "coindesk", "coinmarketcap", "coin bureau",
  136      "blocknews", "decrypt",
  137  ]
  138  
  139  _BREAKING_KEYWORDS = [
  140      "hack", "exploit", "stolen", "breach", "bankrupt", "collapse",
  141      "sec", "cftc", "ban", "lawsuit", "arrest", "sanction", "regulation",
  142      "ath", "all-time high", "etf", "halving",
  143  ]
  144  
  145  
  146  def _fetch_news_rss() -> list[dict]:
  147      """
  148      Fetch stories from RSS feeds. Returns normalised story dicts.
  149      Tries each feed in order; returns combined results from all that succeed.
  150      """
  151      stories: list[dict] = []
  152      for feed_url in _RSS_FEEDS:
  153          try:
  154              feed = feedparser.parse(feed_url)
  155              if feed.bozo and not feed.entries:
  156                  logger.debug("RSS feed parse error for %s: %s", feed_url, feed.bozo_exception)
  157                  continue
  158              source = feed.feed.get("title", feed_url.split("/")[2])
  159              for entry in feed.entries[:15]:
  160                  title = entry.get("title", "").strip()
  161                  link  = entry.get("link", "")
  162                  if not title or not link:
  163                      continue
  164                  stories.append({
  165                      "title":  title,
  166                      "url":    link,
  167                      "source": source,
  168                      "origin": "rss",
  169                  })
  170              logger.debug("RSS %s: %d entries", source, len(feed.entries))
  171          except Exception as exc:
  172              logger.warning("RSS fetch failed for %s: %s", feed_url, exc)
  173  
  174      return stories
  175  
  176  
  177  def _fetch_news_newsapi() -> list[dict]:
  178      """
  179      Fetch stories from NewsAPI.org (fallback).
  180      Returns [] if the request fails.
  181      """
  182      articles = news_api.fetch_crypto_news()
  183      return [dict(a, origin="newsapi") for a in articles]
  184  
  185  
  186  def _fetch_news() -> list[dict]:
  187      """
  188      Fetch the latest stories from both RSS feeds and NewsAPI, then combine
  189      and deduplicate by URL.
  190      """
  191      rss_stories = [dict(s, origin="rss") for s in news_api.fetch_rss_news()]
  192      newsapi_stories = [dict(s, origin="newsapi") for s in news_api.fetch_crypto_news()]
  193  
  194      seen_urls: set[str] = set()
  195      combined: list[dict] = []
  196      for story in rss_stories + newsapi_stories:
  197          url = story.get("url", "")
  198          if url and url in seen_urls:
  199              continue
  200          if url:
  201              seen_urls.add(url)
  202          combined.append(story)
  203  
  204      if not combined:
  205          logger.warning("No news sources available. RSS feeds and NewsAPI both returned nothing.")
  206      return combined
  207  
  208  
  209  # ── Noise filter (pre-AI, fast) ──────────────────────────────────────────────
  210  
  211  def _is_noise(story: dict) -> bool:
  212      """Quick check to reject obviously low-quality stories before AI scoring."""
  213      title = story.get("title", "").lower()
  214  
  215      # Reject noise patterns
  216      for pattern in _NOISE_PATTERNS:
  217          if re.search(pattern, title, re.IGNORECASE):
  218              return True
  219  
  220      # Too short to be meaningful
  221      if len(title) < 20:
  222          return True
  223  
  224      # Macro sources: only keep stories with a crypto/macro bridge keyword
  225      # This prevents random business news from cluttering the feed
  226      if _is_macro_source(story):
  227          if not any(kw in title for kw in _MACRO_CRYPTO_BRIDGE_KEYWORDS):
  228              return True
  229  
  230      return False
  231  
  232  
  233  _GEO_MACRO_KEYWORDS = {
  234      "iran", "china", "russia", "fed", "trump", "tariff", "oil", "dollar",
  235      "sanctions", "war", "reserve", "opec", "yuan", "nato", "treasury",
  236      "inflation", "recession", "powell",
  237      "sec", "blackrock", "fidelity", "coinbase", "binance",
  238      "hack", "exploit", "breach", "bankrupt", "collapse", "arrest", "fraud",
  239      "rate cut", "rate hike", "fomc", "stablecoin bill", "crypto bill",
  240      "clarity act", "etf approved", "etf rejected", "executive order",
  241      "strategic reserve", "bitcoin reserve",
  242      "strait", "hormuz", "blockade", "missile", "nuclear", "ceasefire",
  243      "airstrike", "troops", "military", "pentagon", "conflict", "escalation",
  244      "warship",
  245  }
  246  
  247  
  248  def is_geo_macro_story(story: dict) -> bool:
  249      """Return True if the story title contains a geopolitical/macro keyword."""
  250      title = story.get("title", "").lower()
  251      return any(kw in title for kw in _GEO_MACRO_KEYWORDS)
  252  
  253  
  254  def _is_macro_source(story: dict) -> bool:
  255      """Check if story comes from a macro/geopolitical source."""
  256      # RSS stories from crypto-native feeds are not macro sources.
  257      # CryptoPanic-labelled macro origins still apply.
  258      return story.get("origin", "") in ("reuters_business", "cnbc_economy")
  259  
  260  
  261  def _has_important_keyword(story: dict) -> bool:
  262      """Check if story title contains important keywords."""
  263      title = story.get("title", "").lower()
  264      # Macro sources need a bridge keyword to confirm crypto relevance
  265      if _is_macro_source(story):
  266          return any(kw in title for kw in _MACRO_CRYPTO_BRIDGE_KEYWORDS)
  267      return any(kw in title for kw in _IMPORTANT_KEYWORDS)
  268  
  269  
  270  # ── AI importance scoring + commentary ───────────────────────────────────────
  271  
  272  def _ai_score_and_comment(story: dict) -> dict | None:
  273      """
  274      Use Claude to score story importance (1-10) and write commentary.
  275      Returns story dict with 'score' and 'commentary' added, or None if low quality.
  276      On unexpected errors returns the story with score=5 and empty commentary.
  277      """
  278      try:
  279          import ai_writer
  280  
  281          if not ai_writer.is_available():
  282              # Without AI, use keyword matching as a rough filter
  283              if _has_important_keyword(story):
  284                  score = 8 if _is_priority_source(story) else 7
  285                  story["score"] = score
  286                  story["commentary"] = None  # Will fall back to headline-only
  287                  return story
  288              story["score"] = 5
  289              story["commentary"] = ""
  290              return story
  291  
  292          title = story.get("title", "")
  293          source = story.get("source", "")
  294  
  295          is_macro = _is_macro_source(story)
  296          source_context = ""
  297          if is_macro:
  298              source_context = """
  299  NOTE: This is a MACRO/GEOPOLITICAL story, not crypto-native news.
  300  You MUST connect it to crypto impact. How does this affect BTC, risk assets, liquidity?
  301  If you can't connect it to crypto in a meaningful way, score it low.
  302  When you CAN connect it — this is GOLD content. Macro-to-crypto takes are what
  303  separate a real trader account from a generic crypto news feed."""
  304  
  305          system = f"""You are a crypto news editor for @CoinWatchAlert on Twitter. You decide which stories are worth tweeting and write sharp, opinionated commentary that makes people follow you.
  306  
  307  SCORING (respond with a number 1-10):
  308  - 10: Market-moving (ETF approval/rejection, major hack/exploit, regulatory bombshell, BTC ATH, war/sanctions, Fed surprise)
  309  - 8-9: Very important (major exchange news, institutional moves, rate decisions, breaking regulatory action, SEC/CFTC enforcement, geopolitical shifts)
  310  - 8-10: Political figures + crypto (Trump, White House, Biden, president + Bitcoin/crypto mention)
  311  - 7-9: Executive quotes (Fink, Saylor, CZ, Vitalik, Musk, Dimon, Wood + crypto/BTC take)
  312  - 8-9: Regulatory clarity/bills (crypto regulation bill, law, legislation, bipartisan crypto act)
  313  - 7: Notable (significant market moves, industry trends, macro data, major on-chain events)
  314  - 5-6: Moderately interesting (prediction markets, mid-tier exchange updates, partnership announcements)
  315  - 4: Minor but tweetable (small project updates, minor data points)
  316  - 1-3: Noise (price predictions, sponsored content, repetitive updates, small company treasury adoption)
  317  
  318  CALIBRATION EXAMPLES:
  319  - "BTC drops 10% in 24 hours" → score 7-8
  320  - "ETH outperforming BTC this week" → score 7-8
  321  - "Kraken files for IPO" → score 7-8
  322  - "Polymarket odds shift on election" → score 6-7
  323  - "ECB announces rate decision" / "SEC charges crypto firm" / "Fed signals policy shift" → score 8-10
  324  - "Small company adds BTC to treasury" → score 3-4
  325  - "Crypto influencer makes price prediction" → score 1-2
  326  
  327  AUTOMATIC MINIMUM SCORES — apply these before giving your final score:
  328  - Breaking regulatory news from major bodies (SEC, CFTC, ECB, Fed, ban, lawsuit, arrest, sanction): minimum 8
  329  - Major exchange news (hack, exploit, breach, insolvency, bankruptcy, IPO): minimum 9
  330  - Bitcoin ETF news, BTC/ETH ATH, halving: minimum 8
  331  - Major institutional move (BlackRock, Fidelity, MicroStrategy, sovereign fund): minimum 8
  332  - Fed/Powell/interest rates + crypto or Bitcoin mention: minimum 9
  333  - Trump/White House + crypto or Bitcoin mention: minimum 9
  334  - SEC Chair/Gensler/Atkins + crypto: minimum 9
  335  - Named executive quote (Fink/Saylor/CZ/Vitalik/Musk) + crypto: minimum 8
  336  - Bitcoin ETF flows/inflows/outflows: minimum 8
  337  - Crypto regulation bill/law/legislation: minimum 8
  338  - "Strategic Bitcoin Reserve" any mention: minimum 9
  339  - Hack/exploit over $10M: minimum 9
  340  - Gold/commodities + fear/crash/market cap: minimum 8
  341  - Stock market crash/drop + crypto mention: minimum 8
  342  - "Fear" + market context (VIX, fear index, all-time highs): minimum 7
  343  - Oil price spike + macro implications: minimum 7
  344  - Story comparing crypto market cap to traditional assets: minimum 9
  345  {source_context}
  346  COMMENTARY:
  347  - Write 1-2 punchy sentences with a CLEAR TAKE — bullish or bearish, not neutral
  348  - Include at least ONE specific number: a price level ($X), percentage (X%), dollar amount ($XB), or metric
  349  - Say what this means for price action. Make a call or prediction with a specific target
  350  - For macro news: ALWAYS bridge to crypto — "This means X for BTC because Y"
  351  - Sound like a trader reacting to the news, not a journalist summarizing it
  352  - NEVER write passive commentary like "worth watching" or "interesting development"
  353  - Instead: "This is bullish for BTC because..." or "If this escalates, risk-off sends BTC to $X"
  354  - NO hashtags, NO emojis except 🟢🔴 for direction
  355  - If the story is noise (score < 7), just write "SKIP"
  356  
  357  Format your response EXACTLY like this:
  358  SCORE: [number]
  359  TAKE: [your commentary or SKIP]"""
  360  
  361          prompt = f"""Rate this {'macro/geopolitical' if is_macro else 'crypto'} news story and write commentary:
  362  
  363  Headline: {title}
  364  Source: {source}
  365  
  366  Score it 1-10 and write your take."""
  367  
  368          from ai_writer import _call_claude
  369          result = _call_claude(system, prompt, max_tokens=150)
  370          if not result:
  371              # AI failed — fall back to keyword filter
  372              if _has_important_keyword(story):
  373                  story["score"] = 7
  374                  story["commentary"] = None
  375                  return story
  376              story["score"] = 5
  377              story["commentary"] = ""
  378              return story
  379  
  380          # Parse score
  381          score = 5  # default
  382          score_match = re.search(r'SCORE:\s*(\d+)', result)
  383          if score_match:
  384              score = int(score_match.group(1))
  385  
  386          # Parse commentary
  387          take_match = re.search(r'TAKE:\s*(.+)', result, re.DOTALL)
  388          commentary = None
  389          if take_match:
  390              take = take_match.group(1).strip()
  391              if take.upper() != "SKIP" and len(take) > 10:
  392                  commentary = take
  393  
  394          # Boost priority-source stories by +1 (cap at 10)
  395          if _is_priority_source(story):
  396              score = min(10, score + 1)
  397  
  398          # Boost major institutional/regulatory keywords +2
  399          _major_keywords = {"BlackRock", "Fidelity", "ETF", "SEC", "Fed", "Coinbase"}
  400          if any(kw in title for kw in _major_keywords):
  401              score = min(10, score + 2)
  402  
  403          # Boost crisis/exploit keywords +3
  404          _crisis_keywords = {"hack", "exploit", "bankrupt", "arrest"}
  405          if any(kw in title.lower() for kw in _crisis_keywords):
  406              score = min(10, score + 3)
  407  
  408          story["score"] = score
  409          story["commentary"] = commentary
  410  
  411          if score < 4:
  412              logger.info("News filtered (score %d/10): %.80s", score, title)
  413              return None
  414  
  415          logger.info("News approved (score %d/10): %.80s", score, title)
  416          return story
  417  
  418      except Exception as exc:
  419          logger.warning("_ai_score_and_comment failed for '%.60s': %s",
  420                         story.get("title", ""), exc)
  421          story["score"] = 5
  422          story["commentary"] = ""
  423          return story
  424  
  425  
  426  # ── Public API ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  427  
  428  def check_news() -> list[dict]:
  429      """
  430      Return list of new story dicts that haven't been posted yet.
  431      Side-effect: marks returned stories as seen (persisted to disk).
  432      """
  433      _prune_old_hashes()
  434      stories = _fetch_news()
  435      new_stories = []
  436      added = False
  437      for story in stories:
  438          h = _story_hash(story)
  439          if h not in _posted_hashes:
  440              new_stories.append(story)
  441              _posted_hashes[h] = time.time()
  442              added = True
  443      if added:
  444          _save_posted_hashes()
  445      return new_stories
  446  
  447  
  448  def fetch_latest_headlines(n: int = 3) -> list[str]:
  449      """
  450      Return up to `n` titles from the most recent CryptoPanic stories.
  451      Does NOT mark stories as posted – safe to call for recap generation.
  452      """
  453      stories = _fetch_news()
  454      return [s["title"] for s in stories[:n] if s.get("title")]
  455  
  456  
  457  def format_news_tweet(story: dict) -> str:
  458      """
  459      Turn a scored story dict into a ready-to-post tweet.
  460  
  461      Layout (spaced sections):
  462          🟢 HEADLINE
  463  
  464          [1-2 sentence analyst comment]
  465      """
  466      title = story.get("title", "Breaking crypto news")
  467      commentary = story.get("commentary")
  468      score = story.get("score", 7)
  469  
  470      prefix = _pick_news_prefix(score, title)
  471      headline = f"{prefix} {title}"
  472  
  473      if commentary:
  474          # Trim commentary so full tweet fits within 275 chars
  475          max_commentary = 273 - len(headline) - 2  # "\n\n"
  476          if len(commentary) > max_commentary:
  477              commentary = commentary[:max_commentary - 1].rsplit(" ", 1)[0] + "…"
  478          return f"{headline}\n\n{commentary}"
  479      else:
  480          if len(headline) > 273:
  481              headline = headline[:272].rsplit(" ", 1)[0] + "…"
  482          return headline
  483  
  484  
  485  def get_news_card_type(story: dict) -> str:
  486      """Determine the card type for news image generation."""
  487      score = story.get("score", 7)
  488      title_lower = story.get("title", "").lower()
  489      if score >= 9 or any(w in title_lower for w in ["breaking", "hack", "crash"]):
  490          return "breaking"
  491      if any(w in title_lower for w in ["bull", "rally", "surge", "soar", "pump", "recover"]):
  492          return "bullish"
  493      if any(w in title_lower for w in ["bear", "crash", "dump", "drop", "fall", "plunge"]):
  494          return "bearish"
  495      if any(w in title_lower for w in ["alert", "warning", "risk", "liquidat"]):
  496          return "alert"
  497      return "latest"
  498  
  499  
  500  def fetch_og_image(url: str) -> str | None:
  501      """
  502      Fetch the og:image from an article URL.
  503      Downloads the image to a temp file and returns its path.
  504      Returns None on any failure.
  505      """
  506      if not url:
  507          return None
  508      try:
  509          headers = {"User-Agent": "Mozilla/5.0 (compatible; CryptoBot/1.0)"}
  510          page = requests.get(url, timeout=10, headers=headers)
  511          page.raise_for_status()
  512          soup = BeautifulSoup(page.text, "html.parser")
  513          tag = soup.find("meta", property="og:image") or soup.find(
  514              "meta", attrs={"property": "og:image"}
  515          )
  516          img_url = tag.get("content") if tag else None
  517          if not img_url:
  518              return None
  519          img_resp = requests.get(img_url, timeout=10, headers=headers)
  520          img_resp.raise_for_status()
  521          ct = img_resp.headers.get("Content-Type", "image/jpeg")
  522          ext = ".png" if "png" in ct else ".jpg"
  523          with tempfile.NamedTemporaryFile(delete=False, suffix=ext) as f:
  524              f.write(img_resp.content)
  525              return f.name
  526      except Exception as exc:
  527          logger.debug("OG image fetch failed for %s: %s", url, exc)
  528          return None
  529

  559  _COIN_NAMES = {
  560      "bitcoin": "BTC", "ethereum": "ETH", "solana": "SOL", "bnb": "BNB",
  561      "ripple": "XRP", "xrp": "XRP", "cardano": "ADA", "dogecoin": "DOGE",
  562      "avalanche": "AVAX", "polkadot": "DOT", "chainlink": "LINK",
  563      "polygon": "MATIC", "uniswap": "UNI", "cosmos": "ATOM",
  564      "litecoin": "LTC", "algorand": "ALGO", "near protocol": "NEAR",
  565      "fantom": "FTM", "aptos": "APT", "arbitrum": "ARB",
  566      "optimism": "OP", "sui": "SUI", "injective": "INJ",
  567      "celestia": "TIA", "sei": "SEI", "bittensor": "TAO",
  568      "hyperliquid": "HYPE",
  569  }
  570  
  571  
  572  class NarrativeCluster:
  573      """Groups recent stories by coin/theme in a rolling 6-hour window."""
  574  
  575      WINDOW_SECS = 6 * 3600  # 6 hours
  576      MIN_STORIES = 4          # minimum to flag as emerging
  577  
  578      def __init__(self):
  579          # theme_or_coin -> list of (timestamp, story_dict)
  580          self._buckets: dict[str, list[tuple[float, dict]]] = {}
  581  
  582      def add_story(self, story: dict) -> None:
  583          """Extract topics from a story and file it into buckets."""
  584          now = time.time()
  585          topics = self._extract_topics(story)
  586          for topic in topics:
  587              self._buckets.setdefault(topic, []).append((now, story))
  588          self._prune()
  589  
  590      def _extract_topics(self, story: dict) -> list[str]:
  591          """Return list of coin tickers and theme names found in the story."""
  592          text = (story.get("title", "") + " " + story.get("summary", "")).lower()
  593          topics: list[str] = []
  594  
  595          # Check coin tickers (exact word boundary)
  596          for ticker in _NARRATIVE_COINS:
  597              if re.search(rf'\b{ticker.lower()}\b', text):
  598                  topics.append(ticker)
  599  
  600          # Check full coin names
  601          for name, ticker in _COIN_NAMES.items():
  602              if name in text and ticker not in topics:
  603                  topics.append(ticker)
  604  
  605          # Check macro themes
  606          for theme, keywords in _NARRATIVE_THEMES.items():
  607              if any(kw in text for kw in keywords):
  608                  topics.append(theme)
  609  
  610          return topics
  611  
  612      def _prune(self) -> None:
  613          """Remove entries older than the rolling window."""
  614          cutoff = time.time() - self.WINDOW_SECS
  615          for topic in list(self._buckets):
  616              self._buckets[topic] = [
  617                  (ts, s) for ts, s in self._buckets[topic] if ts > cutoff
  618              ]
  619              if not self._buckets[topic]:
  620                  del self._buckets[topic]
  621  
  622      def get_emerging(self) -> dict | None:
  623          """Return the strongest emerging narrative, or None.
  624  
  625          Uses weighted score: (story_count * 1.5) + (unique_sources * 2) + avg_story_score.
  626          Returns dict with: theme, story_count, sources, summaries, narrative_score.
  627          """
  628          self._prune()
  629          best_topic: str | None = None
  630          best_score = 0.0
  631  
  632          for topic, entries in self._buckets.items():
  633              if len(entries) < self.MIN_STORIES:
  634                  continue
  635              # Compute unique sources
  636              unique_sources = set()
  637              total_story_score = 0
  638              for _, story in entries:
  639                  unique_sources.add(story.get("source", "Unknown"))
  640                  total_story_score += story.get("score", 5)
  641              avg_score = total_story_score / len(entries) if entries else 5
  642              narrative_score = (len(entries) * 1.5) + (len(unique_sources) * 2) + avg_score
  643              if narrative_score > best_score:
  644                  best_score = narrative_score
  645                  best_topic = topic
  646  
  647          if not best_topic:
  648              return None
  649  
  650          entries = self._buckets[best_topic]
  651          seen_titles: set[str] = set()
  652          sources: list[str] = []
  653          summaries: list[str] = []
  654          for _, story in entries:
  655              title = story.get("title", "")
  656              if title in seen_titles:
  657                  continue
  658              seen_titles.add(title)
  659              src = story.get("source", "Unknown")
  660              if src not in sources:
  661                  sources.append(src)
  662              summaries.append(title)
  663  
  664          return {
  665              "theme": best_topic,
  666              "story_count": len(entries),
  667              "narrative_score": round(best_score, 1),
  668              "sources": sources,
  669              "summaries": summaries[:5],  # cap at 5 for prompt brevity
  670          }
  671  
  672  
  673  # Module-level singleton
  674  _narrative_cluster = NarrativeCluster()
  675  
  676  
  677  def feed_narrative(story: dict) -> None:
  678      """Feed a scored story into the narrative clustering engine."""
  679      _narrative_cluster.add_story(story)
  680  
  681  
  682  def check_narratives() -> dict | None:
  683      """Return the strongest emerging narrative if one exists.
  684  
  685      Returns dict: {theme, story_count, sources, summaries} or None.
  686      """
  687      return _narrative_cluster.get_emerging()


================================================================================
  FILE: post_thread.py
================================================================================

    1  #!/usr/bin/env python3
    2  """
    3  Post a one-off Twitter thread immediately.
    4  
    5  Usage:
    6      python post_thread.py                        # uses default topic
    7      python post_thread.py "your topic here"      # custom topic
    8      python post_thread.py --dry-run              # print without posting
    9      python post_thread.py --tweets 6 "topic"     # 6-tweet thread
   10  """
   11  from __future__ import annotations
   12  
   13  import argparse
   14  import logging
   15  import sys
   16  
   17  import ai_writer
   18  import config
   19  import twitter_client
   20  
   21  logging.basicConfig(
   22      level=logging.INFO,
   23      format="%(asctime)s  %(levelname)-8s  %(message)s",
   24      handlers=[logging.StreamHandler(sys.stdout)],
   25  )
   26  logger = logging.getLogger("post_thread")
   27  
   28  DEFAULT_TOPIC = (
   29      "Why crypto market structure has fundamentally changed in 2025-2026: "
   30      "ETF flows, institutional custody, on-chain transparency, and what it means "
   31      "for retail traders watching price action"
   32  )
   33  
   34  
   35  def main() -> None:
   36      parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Post a crypto thread to X/Twitter")
   37      parser.add_argument("topic", nargs="?", default=DEFAULT_TOPIC, help="Thread topic")
   38      parser.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true", help="Print tweets, don't post")
   39      parser.add_argument("--tweets", type=int, default=5, metavar="N", help="Number of tweets (default 5)")
   40      args = parser.parse_args()
   41  
   42      if not config.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY:
   43          logger.critical("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY not set in .env")
   44          sys.exit(1)
   45  
   46      if not args.dry_run:
   47          try:
   48              twitter_client.get_client()
   49          except RuntimeError as exc:
   50              logger.critical("Twitter credentials error: %s", exc)
   51              sys.exit(1)
   52  
   53      logger.info("Generating %d-tweet thread on: %s", args.tweets, args.topic)
   54      tweets = ai_writer.generate_thread(args.topic, n_tweets=args.tweets)
   55  
   56      if not tweets:
   57          logger.error("Thread generation failed — no tweets returned")
   58          sys.exit(1)
   59  
   60      print(f"\n{'─'*60}")
   61      for i, tweet in enumerate(tweets, 1):
   62          print(f"[{i}/{len(tweets)}] ({len(tweet)} chars)\n{tweet}\n")
   63      print('─'*60)
   64  
   65      if args.dry_run:
   66          logger.info("DRY RUN — not posting.")
   67          return
   68  
   69      confirm = input(f"\nPost this {len(tweets)}-tweet thread? [y/N] ").strip().lower()
   70      if confirm != "y":
   71          logger.info("Aborted.")
   72          return
   73  
   74      ok = twitter_client.post_thread(tweets)
   75      if ok:
   76          logger.info("Thread posted successfully.")
   77      else:
   78          logger.error("Thread posting failed (partial post possible — check X).")
   79          sys.exit(1)
   80  
   81  
   82  if __name__ == "__main__":
   83      main()


================================================================================
  FILE: price_monitor.py
================================================================================

    1  """
    2  Price monitor – polls CoinGecko (free, no API key required) and returns
    3  alert objects whenever a coin crosses the configured move thresholds.
    4  """
    5  from __future__ import annotations
    6  
    7  import time
    8  import logging
    9  import requests
   10  
   11  import config
   12  
   13  logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   14  
   15  # Track the last time we fired an alert for each coin so we don't spam.
   16  # Structure: { coin_id: { "1h": timestamp, "24h": timestamp } }
   17  _last_alert: dict[str, dict[str, float]] = {}
   18  
   19  
   20  def _fetch_prices() -> dict | None:
   21      """
   22      Fetch current price + % change for all tracked coins in a single request.
   23      Returns raw CoinGecko market data list, or None on error.
   24      """
   25      coin_ids = ",".join(config.COINS.keys())
   26      url = f"{config.COINGECKO_BASE}/coins/markets"
   27      params = {
   28          "vs_currency": "usd",
   29          "ids": coin_ids,
   30          "price_change_percentage": "1h,24h",
   31          "per_page": len(config.COINS),
   32          "page": 1,
   33      }
   34      try:
   35          resp = requests.get(url, params=params, timeout=15)
   36          resp.raise_for_status()
   37          return resp.json()
   38      except requests.RequestException as exc:
   39          logger.warning("CoinGecko fetch failed: %s", exc)
   40          return None
   41  
   42  
   43  def _cooldown_ok(coin_id: str, window: str) -> bool:
   44      """Return True if we haven't alerted for this coin+window recently."""
   45      now = time.time()
   46      last = _last_alert.get(coin_id, {}).get(window, 0)
   47      return (now - last) >= config.PRICE_ALERT_COOLDOWN
   48  
   49  
   50  def _record_alert(coin_id: str, window: str) -> None:
   51      _last_alert.setdefault(coin_id, {})[window] = time.time()
   52  
   53  
   54  def check_prices() -> list[dict]:
   55      """
   56      Poll CoinGecko and return a list of alert dicts for coins that have
   57      moved beyond the configured thresholds.
   58  
   59      Each alert dict has the keys:
   60          coin_id, symbol, price_usd, pct_change, window, direction
   61      """
   62      data = _fetch_prices()
   63      if not data:
   64          return []
   65  
   66      alerts = []
   67      for coin in data:
   68          coin_id = coin["id"]
   69          symbol  = config.COINS.get(coin_id, coin["symbol"].upper())
   70          price   = coin.get("current_price", 0)
   71  
   72          pct_1h  = coin.get("price_change_percentage_1h_in_currency")
   73          pct_24h = coin.get("price_change_percentage_24h_in_currency")
   74  
   75          if pct_1h is not None and abs(pct_1h) >= config.PRICE_ALERT_1H_PCT:
   76              if _cooldown_ok(coin_id, "1h"):
   77                  alerts.append({
   78                      "coin_id":    coin_id,
   79                      "symbol":     symbol,
   80                      "price_usd":  price,
   81                      "pct_change": pct_1h,
   82                      "window":     "1h",
   83                      "direction":  "up" if pct_1h > 0 else "down",
   84                  })
   85                  _record_alert(coin_id, "1h")
   86  
   87          if pct_24h is not None and abs(pct_24h) >= config.PRICE_ALERT_24H_PCT:
   88              if _cooldown_ok(coin_id, "24h"):
   89                  alerts.append({
   90                      "coin_id":    coin_id,
   91                      "symbol":     symbol,
   92                      "price_usd":  price,
   93                      "pct_change": pct_24h,
   94                      "window":     "24h",
   95                      "direction":  "up" if pct_24h > 0 else "down",
   96                  })
   97                  _record_alert(coin_id, "24h")
   98  
   99      return alerts
  100  
  101  
  102  def _format_price(price: float) -> str:
  103      """Format a price with appropriate decimal places based on magnitude."""
  104      if price >= 1000:
  105          return f"${price:,.2f}"
  106      if price >= 1:
  107          return f"${price:.2f}"
  108      if price >= 0.01:
  109          return f"${price:.4f}"
  110      if price >= 0.0001:
  111          return f"${price:.6f}"
  112      return f"${price:.8f}"
  113  
  114  
  115  def format_price_tweet(alert: dict) -> str:
  116      """Turn a price alert dict into a ready-to-post tweet string."""
  117      arrow     = "🚀" if alert["direction"] == "up" else "🔴"
  118      sign      = "+" if alert["pct_change"] > 0 else ""
  119      pct_str   = f"{sign}{alert['pct_change']:.1f}%"
  120      price_str = _format_price(alert["price_usd"])
  121      window    = alert["window"]
  122  
  123      tweet = (
  124          f"{arrow} #{alert['symbol']} just moved {pct_str} in {window}!\n"
  125          f"Current price: {price_str}\n"
  126          f"#Crypto #Bitcoin #Cryptocurrency"
  127      )
  128      # Hard limit — should never be hit by this template but guard anyway
  129      if len(tweet) > 275:
  130          tweet = tweet[:272] + "…"
  131      return tweet


================================================================================
  FILE: reply_engine.py
================================================================================

    1  """
    2  Reply engine — monitor target accounts and post analytical replies.
    3  
    4  Monitors tweets from configured accounts, generates calm/analytical
    5  replies via ai_writer.generate_reply(), and posts them with optional
    6  coin chart attachments.
    7  
    8  Rate limits: MAX_REPLIES_PER_HOUR, REPLY_MIN_GAP_SECONDS, never
    9  reply to same account twice in a row, persistent replied-IDs store.
   10  """
   11  from __future__ import annotations
   12  
   13  import json
   14  import logging
   15  import os
   16  import random
   17  import time
   18  from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta
   19  
   20  import config
   21  import ai_writer
   22  import twitter_client
   23  import chart_generator
   24  
   25  logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   26  
   27  _DIR = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
   28  _REPLIED_IDS_FILE = os.path.join(_DIR, ".replied_tweet_ids.json")
   29  _MAX_STORED_IDS = 2000
   30  
   31  # ── Persistent store of replied tweet IDs ────────────────────────────────────
   32  
   33  def _load_replied_ids() -> set[str]:
   34      if not os.path.exists(_REPLIED_IDS_FILE):
   35          return set()
   36      try:
   37          with open(_REPLIED_IDS_FILE) as f:
   38              data = json.load(f)
   39          return set(data) if isinstance(data, list) else set()
   40      except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError):
   41          return set()
   42  
   43  
   44  def _save_replied_ids(ids: set[str]) -> None:
   45      # Keep only the most recent IDs to prevent unbounded growth
   46      id_list = sorted(ids)[-_MAX_STORED_IDS:]
   47      try:
   48          with open(_REPLIED_IDS_FILE, "w") as f:
   49              json.dump(id_list, f)
   50      except OSError as exc:
   51          logger.warning("Failed to save replied IDs: %s", exc)
   52  
   53  
   54  # ── Rate limiting state ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   55  
   56  _reply_timestamps: list[float] = []  # timestamps of replies in current hour
   57  _last_reply_time: float = 0.0
   58  _last_replied_account: str = ""
   59  
   60  
   61  def _can_reply() -> bool:
   62      """Check hourly cap and minimum gap."""
   63      global _reply_timestamps
   64      now = time.time()
   65  
   66      # Prune timestamps older than 1 hour
   67      cutoff = now - 3600
   68      _reply_timestamps = [t for t in _reply_timestamps if t > cutoff]
   69  
   70      if len(_reply_timestamps) >= config.MAX_REPLIES_PER_HOUR:
   71          logger.debug("Reply hourly cap (%d) reached.", config.MAX_REPLIES_PER_HOUR)
   72          return False
   73  
   74      if _last_reply_time > 0 and (now - _last_reply_time) < config.REPLY_MIN_GAP_SECONDS:
   75          gap_left = int(config.REPLY_MIN_GAP_SECONDS - (now - _last_reply_time))
   76          logger.debug("Reply min gap — %ds left.", gap_left)
   77          return False
   78  
   79      return True
   80  
   81  
   82  def _record_reply(account: str) -> None:
   83      """Record a reply for rate limiting."""
   84      global _last_reply_time, _last_replied_account
   85      _reply_timestamps.append(time.time())
   86      _last_reply_time = time.time()
   87      _last_replied_account = account
   88  
   89  
   90  # ── Coin detection for chart attachment ──────────────────────────────────────
   91  
   92  # Reuse bot.py's coin map via a lightweight inline version
   93  _COIN_KEYWORDS: dict[str, tuple[str, str]] = {
   94      "BTC": ("bitcoin", "BTC"), "BITCOIN": ("bitcoin", "BTC"),
   95      "ETH": ("ethereum", "ETH"), "ETHEREUM": ("ethereum", "ETH"),
   96      "SOL": ("solana", "SOL"), "SOLANA": ("solana", "SOL"),
   97      "BNB": ("binancecoin", "BNB"), "XRP": ("ripple", "XRP"),
   98      "ADA": ("cardano", "ADA"), "DOGE": ("dogecoin", "DOGE"),
   99      "AVAX": ("avalanche-2", "AVAX"), "LINK": ("chainlink", "LINK"),
  100      "DOT": ("polkadot", "DOT"), "SUI": ("sui", "SUI"),
  101      "ARB": ("arbitrum", "ARB"), "OP": ("optimism", "OP"),
  102      "TAO": ("bittensor", "TAO"), "INJ": ("injective-protocol", "INJ"),
  103  }
  104  
  105  import re as _re
  106  
  107  def _detect_coin(text: str) -> tuple[str, str] | None:
  108      upper = text.upper()
  109      for key in sorted(_COIN_KEYWORDS, key=len, reverse=True):
  110          if len(key) <= 4:
  111              if _re.search(r'(?:\$|\b)' + _re.escape(key) + r'\b', upper):
  112                  return _COIN_KEYWORDS[key]
  113          else:
  114              if key in upper:
  115                  return _COIN_KEYWORDS[key]
  116      return None
  117  
  118  
  119  # ── Core engine ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  120  
  121  def check_and_reply() -> None:
  122      """Main entry point — called by bot.py scheduler every 5 minutes.
  123  
  124      Searches for recent tweets from target accounts, picks the best
  125      candidate, generates a reply, and posts it.
  126      """
  127      logger.info("[REPLY] check_and_reply fired")
  128  
  129      if not config.TWITTER_BEARER_TOKEN:
  130          logger.info("[REPLY] No TWITTER_BEARER_TOKEN — reply engine disabled")
  131          return
  132  
  133      if not _can_reply():
  134          logger.info("[REPLY] Rate limited — skipping this cycle")
  135          return
  136  
  137      accounts = config.REPLY_ACCOUNTS
  138      if not accounts:
  139          logger.info("[REPLY] No REPLY_ACCOUNTS configured — skipping")
  140          return
  141  
  142      logger.info("[REPLY] Monitoring %d accounts: %s", len(accounts), ", ".join(f"@{a}" for a in accounts))
  143  
  144      replied_ids = _load_replied_ids()
  145      logger.info("[REPLY] Loaded %d previously replied tweet IDs", len(replied_ids))
  146      now_utc = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
  147      max_age = timedelta(minutes=10)
  148  
  149      # Build search query: from any target account, exclude retweets
  150      query_parts = [f"from:{acct}" for acct in accounts]
  151      query = f"({' OR '.join(query_parts)}) -is:retweet"
  152      logger.info("[REPLY] Search query: %s", query)
  153  
  154      try:
  155          tweets = twitter_client.search_recent_tweets(query, max_results=20)
  156          logger.info("[REPLY] Search returned %d tweets", len(tweets) if tweets else 0)
  157      except Exception as exc:
  158          logger.error("[REPLY] API error searching tweets: %s", exc)
  159          return
  160  
  161      if not tweets:
  162          logger.info("[REPLY] No recent tweets from target accounts")
  163          return
  164  
  165      # Filter candidates
  166      candidates = []
  167      for tweet in tweets:
  168          tid = tweet["id"]
  169          text = tweet.get("text", "")
  170          author = tweet.get("author_id", "")
  171          created = tweet.get("created_at")
  172  
  173          # Already replied
  174          if tid in replied_ids:
  175              logger.debug("[REPLY] Skip %s — already replied", tid)
  176              continue
  177  
  178          # Too old
  179          if created and (now_utc - created) > max_age:
  180              age_mins = (now_utc - created).total_seconds() / 60
  181              logger.debug("[REPLY] Skip %s — too old (%.0fm)", tid, age_mins)
  182              continue
  183  
  184          # Too short (skip under 10 words)
  185          if len(text.split()) < 10:
  186              logger.debug("[REPLY] Skip %s — too short (%d words)", tid, len(text.split()))
  187              continue
  188  
  189          # Never reply to same account twice in a row
  190          if author == _last_replied_account:
  191              logger.debug("[REPLY] Skip %s — same account as last reply", tid)
  192              continue
  193  
  194          logger.info("[REPLY] Eligible candidate: %s | %.60s", tid, text)
  195          candidates.append(tweet)
  196  
  197      if not candidates:
  198          logger.info("[REPLY] No eligible candidates after filtering %d tweets", len(tweets))
  199          return
  200  
  201      logger.info("[REPLY] %d eligible candidates found", len(candidates))
  202  
  203      # Pick the most recent candidate
  204      candidate = candidates[0]
  205      tweet_text = candidate["text"]
  206      tweet_id = candidate["id"]
  207      author_id = candidate.get("author_id", "unknown")
  208  
  209      logger.info("[REPLY] Generating reply to tweet %s from author %s: %.80s",
  210                  tweet_id, author_id, tweet_text)
  211  
  212      # Generate reply
  213      reply_text = ai_writer.generate_reply(tweet_text)
  214      if not reply_text:
  215          logger.warning("[REPLY] generate_reply returned None for tweet %s", tweet_id)
  216          return
  217  
  218      logger.info("[REPLY] Generated reply: %.100s", reply_text)
  219  
  220      # Generate chart if coin detected
  221      chart_path: str | None = None
  222      coin = _detect_coin(tweet_text)
  223      if coin:
  224          logger.info("[REPLY] Coin detected: %s — generating chart", coin[1])
  225          try:
  226              chart_path = chart_generator.generate_line_fill(coin[0], coin[1], 1)
  227          except Exception as exc:
  228              logger.warning("[REPLY] Chart generation failed: %s", exc)
  229      else:
  230          logger.info("[REPLY] No coin detected — text-only reply")
  231  
  232      # Random delay 30-120 seconds before posting
  233      delay = random.randint(30, 120)
  234      logger.info("[REPLY] Waiting %ds before posting reply to %s", delay, tweet_id)
  235      time.sleep(delay)
  236  
  237      # Re-check rate limit after delay
  238      if not _can_reply():
  239          logger.info("[REPLY] Rate limit hit after delay — skipping")
  240          return
  241  
  242      # Post reply — calls twitter_client.post_tweet directly (bypasses bot._emit)
  243      # Replies have their own rate limiting via _can_reply / _reply_timestamps
  244      logger.info("[REPLY] post_tweet function ref: %s", twitter_client.post_tweet)
  245      logger.info("[REPLY] About to call post_tweet: reply_to=%s, text_len=%d, chart=%s",
  246                  tweet_id, len(reply_text), bool(chart_path))
  247      try:
  248          posted = twitter_client.post_tweet(
  249              reply_text,
  250              image_path=chart_path,
  251              in_reply_to_tweet_id=tweet_id,
  252          )
  253          logger.info("[REPLY] post_tweet returned: %s", posted)
  254  
  255          if posted:
  256              _record_reply(author_id)
  257              replied_ids.add(tweet_id)
  258              _save_replied_ids(replied_ids)
  259              snippet = reply_text[:60].replace("\n", " ")
  260              logger.info("[REPLY] Successfully posted reply to %s: %s", tweet_id, snippet)
  261          else:
  262              logger.error("[REPLY] Failed to post reply to %s — post_tweet returned False", tweet_id)
  263      except Exception as exc:
  264          logger.error("[REPLY] Exception posting reply to %s: %s", tweet_id, exc)


================================================================================
  FILE: reply_tool.py
================================================================================

    1  #!/usr/bin/env python3
    2  """
    3  reply_tool.py – Interactive Twitter/X reply generator for crypto content.
    4  
    5  Workflow
    6  ────────
    7  1. Fetch the 10 most recent tweets from four major crypto accounts.
    8  2. Display them (account, text, likes).
    9  3. You pick one by number.
   10  4. Claude generates an analyst-voice reply (max 220 chars, no NFA, no !).
   11  5. You confirm Y/N before it's posted.
   12  
   13  Usage:
   14      cd ~/crypto-bot-
   15      python reply_tool.py
   16  """
   17  from __future__ import annotations
   18  
   19  import os
   20  import sys
   21  import textwrap
   22  
   23  # ── Resolve paths ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   24  ROOT = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
   25  sys.path.insert(0, ROOT)
   26  
   27  import config as cfg
   28  
   29  # ── Constants ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   30  TARGET_ACCOUNTS = ["CoinDesk", "Cointelegraph", "WatcherGuru", "BitcoinMagazine"]
   31  TWEETS_PER_ACCT = 3          # fetch up to N per account (3 × 4 = 12 > 10 shown)
   32  REPLY_MAX_CHARS = 220
   33  ALLOWED_EMOJIS  = "🚀📉⚡👀"
   34  
   35  CLAUDE_SYSTEM = f"""\
   36  You are a professional crypto market analyst with 10 years of experience.
   37  Write a concise, insightful reply to the tweet below.
   38  
   39  Rules (strictly enforced):
   40  - Maximum {REPLY_MAX_CHARS} characters (count carefully).
   41  - No exclamation marks (!).
   42  - Do NOT include "NFA", "DYOR", "not financial advice", or any disclaimer.
   43  - Only use these emojis if they genuinely add value: {ALLOWED_EMOJIS}
   44  - Write in first person, confident analyst voice.
   45  - No hashtags unless already in the original tweet.
   46  - Return ONLY the reply text — no quotes, no preamble.
   47  """
   48  
   49  TWEET_LOG = os.path.join(ROOT, "posted_tweets.log")
   50  
   51  
   52  # ── Dependency helpers ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   53  
   54  def _require(pkg: str, import_name: str | None = None):
   55      """Import a package, showing a helpful error if missing."""
   56      try:
   57          import importlib
   58          return importlib.import_module(import_name or pkg)
   59      except ImportError:
   60          print(f"\n[ERROR] Required package '{pkg}' not installed.")
   61          print(f"        Run:  pip install {pkg}")
   62          sys.exit(1)
   63  
   64  
   65  # ── Twitter fetch ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   66  
   67  def _twitter_client():
   68      """Return a Tweepy v2 Client using credentials from config."""
   69      tweepy = _require("tweepy")
   70      missing = [k for k, v in [
   71          ("TWITTER_BEARER_TOKEN",        cfg.TWITTER_BEARER_TOKEN),
   72          ("TWITTER_API_KEY",             cfg.TWITTER_API_KEY),
   73          ("TWITTER_API_SECRET",          cfg.TWITTER_API_SECRET),
   74          ("TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN",        cfg.TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN),
   75          ("TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN_SECRET", cfg.TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN_SECRET),
   76      ] if not v]
   77      if missing:
   78          print(f"\n[ERROR] Missing Twitter credentials in .env: {', '.join(missing)}")
   79          sys.exit(1)
   80      return tweepy.Client(
   81          bearer_token=cfg.TWITTER_BEARER_TOKEN,
   82          consumer_key=cfg.TWITTER_API_KEY,
   83          consumer_secret=cfg.TWITTER_API_SECRET,
   84          access_token=cfg.TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN,
   85          access_token_secret=cfg.TWITTER_ACCESS_TOKEN_SECRET,
   86          wait_on_rate_limit=True,
   87      )
   88  
   89  
   90  def fetch_recent_tweets() -> list[dict]:
   91      """
   92      Fetch up to TWEETS_PER_ACCT recent tweets from each TARGET_ACCOUNTS.
   93      Returns a list of dicts: {id, account, text, like_count, url}.
   94      """
   95      tweepy  = _require("tweepy")
   96      client  = _twitter_client()
   97      results = []
   98  
   99      for username in TARGET_ACCOUNTS:
  100          try:
  101              user_resp = client.get_user(
  102                  username=username,
  103                  user_fields=["public_metrics"],
  104              )
  105              if not user_resp.data:
  106                  print(f"  @{username}: user not found")
  107                  continue
